| IntegrationMapping                                                             | Integration service and super administrators; scoped admins for approved provider mappings                                   | Integration service/authorized administrators                                                                | Revoke/supersede; delete unused draft only                                              | Not applicable                                                             | Super Administrator                           | External identifiers can enable cross-system data leakage if broadly visible.                            |

## 11.1 RLS design rules

1. Academy scope is mandatory for academy-owned data.
2. Student access requires subject ownership plus enrollment/journey/delivery rules where relevant.
3. Instructor access requires current InstructorAssignment or explicit review assignment.
4. Sponsor access resolves through active SponsorRelationship, consent version and field-visibility policy.
5. AnswerDefinition, protected prompts, unpublished forms and raw AI governance records are denied to students and sponsors.
6. Services identify both service purpose and originating actor/correlation where available.
7. Security-definer or bypass mechanisms, if physically required, are isolated, reviewed and never callable generically from clients.
8. Every policy has positive and negative tests: same subject, same academy different student, different academy, expired assignment, suspended enrollment, revoked sponsor, unpublished object and guessed identifier.
9. Exports and object-storage access enforce equivalent row and field rules.
10. RLS policies must not depend on mutable untrusted client claims for consequential access.

# SECTION 12 — VERSIONING STRATEGY

| Versioned concern              | Strategy                                                                      | New version trigger                                                                                                    | Historical binding                                                                       | Correction / retirement                                                           |
| ------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Courses                        | Stable Course identity with immutable CourseVersion sequence.                 | Title/capability/curriculum/exam-alignment changes after publication.                                                  | Offering pins exact version; existing enrollments stay pinned unless explicit migration. | Retire version; do not overwrite.                                                 |
| Questions                      | Stable QuestionItem with immutable QuestionVersion.                           | Prompt, options, answer, explanation, stimulus link, competency mapping or response schema change.                     | Forms and attempts reference exact version.                                              | Withdraw defective version; impact analysis and regrade decision.                 |
| Competencies                   | CompetencyFrameworkVersion owns immutable competency hierarchy.               | Meaning, code hierarchy, observable indicators or material mappings change.                                            | Evidence retains original competency/version; crosswalks support comparison.             | Supersede framework; mappings versioned.                                          |
| Assessments                    | AssessmentDefinition stable; immutable AssessmentVersion, Blueprint and Form. | Timing, scoring, coverage, release, item composition or attempt policy change.                                         | Delivery pins form/version; attempts never follow latest.                                | Withdraw/retire; create corrected version/form.                                   |
| Learning Paths                 | LearningPathTemplate versions plus journey-specific LearningPath revisions.   | Goal/baseline/curriculum/path logic or required step changes.                                                          | Completed steps remain; active revision references predecessor and change rationale.     | Supersede revision; migrate only compatible incomplete steps.                     |
| Rubrics                        | Stable rubric code with immutable effective Rubric version.                   | Criterion, descriptor, scale, aggregation or interpretation change.                                                    | Every GradeRun references exact version.                                                 | Regrade only through approved impact decision.                                    |
| Recommendations                | RecommendationPolicyVersion and immutable individual Recommendation.          | Candidate rules, ranking, constraints or model change creates policy version; new evidence creates new recommendation. | Existing recommendation retains policy/evidence/rationale; may expire or be superseded.  | Do not rewrite reasons or rank history.                                           |
| AI Models                      | Immutable AIModelVersion and AIPromptVersion approved per capability.         | Provider/model/config/prompt/output schema/safety or benchmark package change.                                         | Every AI run pins both versions plus rubric/context policy.                              | Suspend immediately; route/retry with new run/version; never relabel old outputs. |
| Study Plans                    | Journey-specific numbered StudyPlan revisions.                                | Material date, workload, goal, path or schedule changes.                                                               | Past activities remain linked to historical revision; one current active revision.       | Supersede; do not delete adherence history.                                       |
| Journey snapshots              | Immutable sequence/event-time snapshots.                                      | Created on material journey, goal, path, plan or readiness milestones.                                                 | Each snapshot points to exact active versions at effective time.                         | Invalid snapshot can be superseded with correction note.                          |
| Exam specifications            | Stable ExamProduct with immutable ExamProductVersion and score scales.        | Official structure, scoring, tools or policy change.                                                                   | Courses, simulations, goals and results pin exact applicable version.                    | Supersede by effective dates; never silently remap history.                       |
| Curricula and learning content | Stable course/curriculum identities with immutable publication packages.      | Objectives, order, prerequisites, content or asset changes after publication.                                          | Enrollments pin course/curriculum context; resource corrections use version lineage.     | Retire/withdraw; approved migration plan for active learners.                     |
| Practice policies              | Immutable PracticePolicyVersion.                                              | Selection, exposure, feedback or mastery-evidence policy change.                                                       | Session records policy version and selected item set.                                    | New sessions use new version; existing sessions finish under pinned policy.       |
| Simulation policies            | Immutable SimulationPolicyPack version.                                       | Timing, routing, break, tool, media or accommodation policy change.                                                    | Session pins pack before start.                                                          | Suspend/withdraw definition if fidelity defect.                                   |
| Readiness models               | Immutable ReadinessModelVersion.                                              | Inputs, weights, minimum evidence, confidence, score conversion or state thresholds change.                            | Snapshots retain model/evidence boundary.                                                | Recalculate prospectively or in a labelled backtest; do not overwrite history.    |
| Academic metrics               | Immutable AcademicMetricDefinition version.                                   | Formula, population, exclusions, timezone or source lineage change.                                                    | Snapshots/reports display metric version.                                                | Recompute only as a new reporting series/version.                                 |

## 12.1 Version identity principles

- Stable identity answers “what lineage is this?”; version answers “which exact approved definition was used?”
- Published/effective versions are immutable.
- `latest` is a query convenience, never a stored historical relationship.
- Effective periods and supersession are explicit.
- Versions contain semantic numbers or labels for governance, while identifiers remain the technical reference.
- A version manifest includes dependency versions and a content/configuration hash.
- Cross-version migration is a business command with impact analysis, not a foreign-key replacement script.
- Model, prompt, rubric, score scale and evidence boundary are part of academic result provenance.

# SECTION 13 — AUDIT STRATEGY

Audit is distinct from operational logging and domain event streaming.

## 13.1 Audit classes

| Class                      | Examples                                                                                                                                          | Audience                                                          | Retention principle                                                                            | Payload minimization                                                                        |
| -------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Security events            | Login success/failure; verification; MFA; recovery; session revocation; role change; impersonation; suspicious access; secret/policy change       | Security/Audit personnel; affected-user notices where appropriate | Security policy, typically long and tamper-resistant; exact years set by jurisdiction/contract | No tokens, passwords, recovery secrets or unnecessary personal content.                     |
| Academic governance events | Question/rubric/form/model review; approval; publication; withdrawal; regrade; grade override; result verification                                | Academic governance, auditors, authorized administrators          | At least through content/result dispute and academic retention horizon                         | Preserve actor, role, gate, reason, dependency versions and before/after summary.           |
| Academic learner events    | Enrollment transitions; journey stage; diagnostic; attempt submission; grade release; evidence invalidation; readiness and intervention decisions | Student subject views where appropriate; assigned staff; auditors | Long-term academic record or de-identified analytical retention                                | Do not place full essays, recordings or answers in audit payload.                           |
| AI events                  | Model/prompt activation; run status; validation; confidence; review route; override; provider incident; cost/latency summary                      | AI governance, academic reviewers, audit                          | Through model governance, dispute and compliance period; raw payload retention may be shorter  | Use references/hashes for source and output; redact provider secrets and excessive content. |
| Administrative events      | User/staff changes; bulk import/export; configuration; academy suspension; destructive/archival action                                            | Scoped administrators, audit                                      | Long enough for operational accountability and contractual requirements                        | Purpose/reason mandatory for sensitive exports and overrides.                               |
| System events              | Deployment; migration; queue/job failures; backup/restore; incident response; configuration checksum                                              | Platform Operations, security, audit                              | Operational retention plus archived incident records                                           | Operational telemetry is separate; audit records only material system actions.              |

## 13.2 Canonical audit record

Required logical fields:

- Audit event identifier.
- Occurred and recorded time.
- Actor identity or service identity.
- Original actor when impersonation or delegated service execution occurs.
- Academy and authorization scope.
- Action code.
- Entity type, identifier and version.
- Command/correlation/request identifiers.
- Outcome: succeeded, rejected or failed.
- Previous and resulting lifecycle state.
- Change summary; protected fields redacted or hashed.
- Reason and approval reference where required.
- Source channel, risk context and network/device metadata where lawful.
- Retention class and legal-hold indicator.

## 13.3 Integrity and access

Audit events are append-only. Corrections append a clarifying event. Access is read-only and segregated. High-risk audit streams should be exported to an independent tamper-resistant archive/SIEM. Clock synchronization, correlation propagation and hash/checksum strategies are operational requirements. Audit access itself is audited.

## 13.4 Retention governance

Exact periods cannot be finalized until applicable jurisdictions, academy contracts, age groups and dispute requirements are approved. Phase 1 must establish a retention schedule by data class and country. Retention jobs must honor legal holds, active disputes, sponsor/guardian rules and de-identification requirements.

# SECTION 14 — FILE & MEDIA ARCHITECTURE

| Media class               | Use                                                              | Logical representation                                                                                             | Access model                                                                                   | Validation and processing                                                                            | Retention/versioning                                                                   |
| ------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Images                    | Question diagrams, course graphics, profile or publication media | FileAsset reference with immutable version, dimensions, alt text, rights/provenance, sensitivity and hash          | Private by default for protected academic assets; public/CDN only after publication policy     | MIME/signature validation, malware scan, image safety processing, accessibility metadata             | Follow owning content; old versions retained while referenced                          |
| Audio                     | Listening stimuli, pronunciation samples and feedback            | Original master plus derived delivery renditions; transcript/captions as separate versioned assets                 | Private signed delivery; protected stimuli never public object paths                           | Codec/duration validation, malware scan, loudness/quality checks, rights metadata                    | Content policy; attempts retain exact referenced version                               |
| Videos                    | Lessons and demonstrations                                       | Master asset plus transcoded renditions, captions, poster and playback metadata                                    | Private streaming or controlled CDN; entitlement checked                                       | Scan, transcode isolation, caption/accessibility validation, rights/provenance                       | Course/content retention and lifecycle policies                                        |
| Reading passages          | Structured text plus optional source PDF/image assets            | Prefer canonical structured content with versioned formatting; source file retained for provenance where permitted | Delivered through authorized content/form projections                                          | Academic rights review, encoding/accessibility validation, plagiarism/provenance process             | Immutable published versions; attempts pin exact version                               |
| PDFs                      | Learning resources, reports and evidence documents               | FileAsset plus extracted metadata/accessible version where applicable                                              | Private unless explicitly published; signed access                                             | MIME/signature, malware scan, size/page limits, accessibility review for published learning PDFs     | Owner and legal policy; generated exports expire separately                            |
| Essays                    | Student-authored submission text and attachments                 | Submission stores immutable text snapshot/hash and references original asset if uploaded                           | Restricted to student, assigned graders and authorized staff; sponsor only by explicit consent | Type/size validation, malware scan for attachments, content normalization without replacing original | Long-term academic/dispute retention; AI provider copies minimized                     |
| Speaking recordings       | Student audio/video speaking submissions                         | Original immutable recording, normalized derivative, transcript and acoustic-analysis references                   | Restricted private storage and short-lived access                                              | Format/duration validation, scan, audio quality checks, consent notice                               | Academic/dispute retention with stricter privacy; provider copies deleted per contract |
| AI outputs                | Structured grade, feedback, prediction or assistant output       | Validated canonical output stored with GradeRun/AI run; raw provider payload separately restricted if retained     | Academic roles and student released view according to policy                                   | Schema/safety validation, model/prompt/context provenance, redaction                                 | Canonical decision retained; raw payload shorter unless dispute/legal need             |
| Certificates              | Generated evidence of academy-defined completion/achievement     | Immutable certificate manifest, template version, recipient/outcome references and verification code               | Recipient and authorized sponsor/staff; public verification reveals minimal fields             | Template approval, cryptographic/hash verification, revocation support                               | Retain while credential valid plus archive; revocation history permanent               |
| Official result evidence  | Uploaded score report or verification response                   | Restricted FileAsset linked to OfficialResult verification                                                         | Student and authorized verifier/admin only; sponsor summary not raw file by default            | Scan, format validation, authenticity workflow, minimal extraction                                   | High-sensitivity retention; delete/de-identify when no longer required by policy       |
| Generated reports/exports | CSV/PDF/other report delivery                                    | Ephemeral export asset with requester, scope, query/metric versions and expiry                                     | One-time or short-lived authorized access                                                      | Server-side authorization, row/field filtering, scan where generated externally                      | Short retention; automatic deletion after expiry unless formally archived              |

## 14.1 Asset lifecycle

```text
upload_pending
→ quarantined
→ scanning
→ approved
→ active
→ superseded / retired
→ disposed when unreferenced and retention permits
```

Failed or suspicious assets move to `quarantined_rejected`; they are never rendered to users.

## 14.2 Storage principles

1. Database stores metadata and opaque asset reference; object storage stores bytes.
2. Original and derived assets are distinct versions with lineage.
3. Object paths are not authorization mechanisms.
4. Signed upload and delivery grants are short-lived and bound to intended operation.
5. Hashes support deduplication and integrity but do not merge files across privacy/ownership boundaries automatically.
6. No file is considered active until scan and ownership checks pass.
7. Deleting a parent object cannot orphan or prematurely erase an asset referenced elsewhere.
8. Provider-specific storage details are hidden behind the File Asset contract.

# SECTION 15 — API CONTRACT MODEL

APIs expose domain capabilities, not physical tables. The initial deployment may be a modular monolith, but contracts must permit later service extraction. Public APIs serve web/mobile/institution clients; internal APIs serve trusted modules/workers; events communicate completed facts.

| Academic Intelligence Service | Governed metrics and analytical projections                            | Audience-specific reports and exports                                                     | Metric governance and projection rebuild                       | AcademicIntelligenceUpdated                             | Warehouse/BI adapters                   |
| Academic Operations Service   | Cross-domain work queues and commands                                  | Staff operations views                                                                    | Orchestrates authorized domain commands; owns no foreign state | OperationalTaskCreated/Completed                        | Admin BFF                               |
| Communication Service         | Announcements, templates, preferences and deliveries                   | Read audience announcements; manage own preferences                                       | Publish/withdraw and dispatch                                  | NotificationDelivered; AnnouncementPublished            | Email/SMS/push adapters                 |
| Audit Service                 | Append-only audit and investigations                                   | Formal subject/security notices only                                                      | Append and authorized query/export                             | AuditRecorded                                           | SIEM/archive                            |
| Integration Service           | External mappings, imports and webhooks                                | No general public CRUD                                                                    | Validate/map/import/export/webhook handling                    | ExternalMappingCreated; ImportCompleted                 | Provider anti-corruption layers         |
| Platform Operations Service   | Health, queues, jobs, incidents and feature controls                   | Status page where applicable                                                              | Operational commands and recovery                              | PlatformIncidentOpened; JobFailed                       | Cloud/observability providers           |

## 15.2 Contract rules

- Public contracts are versioned and use canonical vocabulary, not provider or table names.
- Command endpoints express intent; clients cannot submit arbitrary lifecycle status.
- Query endpoints use cursor pagination and allow-listed filters.
- Idempotency is mandatory for registration, enrollment, attempt start, autosave, submission, external webhooks, payment/result imports and job scheduling.
- Public error contracts distinguish validation, authorization, state conflict, version conflict, quota, dependency and transient failure.
- Student assessment contracts contain only delivery-safe content.
- Internal APIs use service identity, purpose and academy/resource scope.
- Long-running imports, exports, AI and analytical work return job references and complete asynchronously.
- Events are preferred over synchronous chains for derived intelligence and notifications.
- Synchronous calls are reserved for business decisions that must be known before committing the initiating command.
- Contract tests are mandatory for every consumer/provider pair.

## 15.3 Public versus internal boundaries

Public does not mean anonymous. “Public API” means supported client-facing contract. Most endpoints require authentication. Internal APIs are not automatically trusted; they authenticate service identity and validate scope. Direct database APIs are not the canonical business API, though constrained read views/functions may implement approved contracts physically.

# SECTION 16 — MIGRATION STRATEGY

Migration must preserve current operations while replacing legacy conceptual debt. The target is a controlled strangler migration, not a one-time rewrite with unverified data conversion.

## 16.1 Reusable foundations

Likely reusable after validation:

- Existing authentication identities and verified contact details.
- Academy/staff/user profile data with canonical-person reconciliation.
- Existing exam product references where semantics are clear.
- Published course/catalogue content that can be mapped to Course, CourseVersion and Offering.
- Existing relational test forms, sections, passages, question groups, questions, answer keys, attempts and responses where exact versions and ownership are reconstructable.
- Existing object-storage assets after hash, ownership, scan and rights inventory.
- Existing enrollment and program-practice linkage where source meaning is unambiguous.
- Existing result records that can be tied to attempt, student, course/offering and scoring version.

“Reusable” means reusable data or capability, not automatic reuse of existing table boundaries.

## 16.2 Domains requiring redesign

| Legacy area                     | Required target redesign                                                                                               |
| ------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| User role/profile               | Canonical person, academy membership and scoped RoleAssignment                                                         |
| Programs/courses/practice tasks | ExamProduct, Course, CourseVersion, CourseOffering, Enrollment and typed capabilities                                  |
| Student progress                | PreparationJourney, LearningPath, StudyPlan, evidence and projections                                                  |
| Practice                        | Adaptive Practice using shared Delivery Kernel                                                                         |
| Assessment/mock separation      | Assessment Definition plus Exam Simulation policies over shared Attempt/Response/Submission                            |
| Question content                | Stable QuestionItem, immutable QuestionVersion, reusable Stimulus, restricted AnswerDefinition and competency mappings |
| Results                         | GradeRun, GradeDecision, AcademicEvidence, Mastery and Readiness separation                                            |
| AI grading                      | Provider-neutral model/prompt/rubric provenance and review workflow                                                    |
| Admin                           | Academic Operations Center and academic publishing gates                                                               |
| Analytics                       | Governed event projections and Academic Intelligence                                                                   |
| Files                           | Canonical FileAsset metadata, privacy, version and owner references                                                    |

## 16.3 Legacy concepts to retire or translate

- Global role strings on profiles.
- Course-specific or exam-specific duplicate result/attempt tables.
- “Practice versus mock” as separate persistence engines.
- Mutable published questions or tests.
- Direct student linkage to a test without enrollment/journey context.
- Completion percentage as readiness.
- Generic status fields changed by UI.
- Unrestricted JSON used for relational ownership.
- Hard-coded dashboard per exam product.
- Answer keys included in broad test-form payloads.
- AI score fields with no model, rubric, prompt or review provenance.
- Duplicate active test forms or practice-task links with unclear authority.
- Physical file paths as durable business identifiers.

## 16.4 Migration order

1. Freeze and document legacy vocabulary, schemas, flows and active incidents.
2. Inventory identities and create canonical person/academy mapping.
3. Establish target academy, membership, role and authorization foundations.
4. Map exam products, score scales and existing competency/taxonomy data.
5. Map courses, versions, offerings, cohorts and instructor assignments.
6. Reconstruct enrollments and pin academic versions.
7. Create PreparationJourneys and initial goals for active learners using explicit inference rules and review queues.
8. Migrate content and assets, preserving provenance and hashes.
9. Migrate question lineage, stimuli, answer definitions, rubrics and publication state.
10. Migrate assessment definitions, forms, deliveries, attempts, responses and submissions.
11. Reconstruct GradeDecisions from legacy results; preserve raw legacy score references.
12. Generate normalized AcademicEvidence from validated migrated outcomes.
13. Initialize mastery/readiness only after model approval; mark insufficient evidence where appropriate.
14. Build projections for workspace, timeline, operations and analytics.
15. Reconcile counts, referential integrity, answer-key security, student histories and score totals.
16. Run shadow reads and selected dual-run comparisons; avoid indefinite dual writes.
17. Cut over domain by domain behind feature flags.
18. Place legacy system in read-only mode.
19. Complete final reconciliation, retention/archive and decommission plan.

## 16.5 Data migration mechanics

- Use `IntegrationMapping`/migration crosswalk records for every legacy-to-canonical identity.
- Every batch has source checksum, transform version, run identifier, actor, timestamps and per-record outcome.
- Dry run precedes commit.
- Imports are idempotent.
- Ambiguous records enter review queues; they are not guessed silently.
- Source data remains immutable during rehearsal.
- Reconciliation validates totals and semantic samples, not only row counts.
- Media migration validates actual bytes, references and permissions.
- Answer keys receive separate security verification.
- Active attempts require a special cutover policy: finish in legacy, freeze and migrate, or controlled replay; never split one attempt across engines without design.

## 16.6 Backward compatibility

Compatibility is provided through adapters and read-only legacy identifiers, not by preserving flawed target concepts. During transition:

- Legacy URLs may resolve through mapping to canonical resources.
- Existing clients may use versioned compatibility APIs for a defined sunset period.
- Events may be translated to legacy notifications where required.
- New writes should move to canonical domains as each slice is cut over.
- Dual writing is temporary, monitored and reconciled; it has an explicit end date.
- Database views may support reporting compatibility but are not allowed to become new sources of truth.

## 16.7 Migration risks

| Risk                              | Impact                           | Mitigation                                                                |
| --------------------------------- | -------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Duplicate identities              | Cross-student data contamination | Canonical matching rules, manual review and reversible merge              |
| Ambiguous course/test ownership   | Incorrect entitlement/history    | Source-to-target ownership matrix and sampled academic review             |
| Mutable legacy questions          | Historical attempt mismatch      | Snapshot/hash reconstruction and exception report                         |
| Missing answer/version provenance | Unreproducible grades            | Preserve legacy raw record; classify confidence; do not fabricate version |
| Duplicate test forms/tasks        | Conflicting student visibility   | Authority rules, deduplication and administrator review                   |
| Incomplete responses or grading   | Loss of history                  | State-aware migration and explicit unresolved status                      |
| Answer-key exposure               | Critical security failure        | Isolated migration path and negative authorization tests                  |
| Media orphans                     | Broken content/submissions       | Reference graph and hash-based reconciliation                             |
| AI fields without provenance      | Unverifiable evaluation          | Migrate as legacy grade source; do not relabel as governed AI run         |
| Large batch load                  | Production disruption            | Throttled workers, off-peak windows and rollback checkpoints              |
| Indefinite dual systems           | Divergent truth                  | Domain cutover plan, reconciliation metrics and sunset governance         |

## 16.8 Migration acceptance

A domain cutover is accepted only when identity mapping, counts, referential checks, state distribution, sample histories, authorization negatives, score reproduction, asset access, audit and rollback have passed agreed tolerances. Tolerances must be approved before execution.

# SECTION 17 — CANONICAL BUSINESS VOCABULARY

All teams, APIs, database designs, reports and interfaces must use these terms consistently. Synonyms may be used only as localized UI labels while canonical contracts retain the terms below.

| Term                       | Canonical definition                                                                                                  |
| -------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Academic Evidence          | A normalized, traceable observation of learner performance or completion used by mastery/readiness models.            |
| Academic Intelligence      | Governed analysis that helps the academy improve questions, courses, AI, instruction, readiness and outcomes.         |
| Academic Operations Center | The staff experience for governing academic production, delivery, quality, exceptions and student outcomes.           |
| Academic Publishing        | Risk-tiered process that reviews, approves, versions, publishes, retires and archives academic assets.                |
| Academy                    | The business tenant that owns courses, memberships, enrollments and academy-scoped operations.                        |
| Achievement                | A verified milestone generated from governed criteria and qualifying evidence.                                        |
| Adaptive Practice          | Formative practice whose item selection responds to mastery, difficulty, recency, exposure, goals and evidence needs. |
| Aggregate                  | A consistency boundary changed through one aggregate root.                                                            |
| Aggregate Root             | The object through which commands enforce all invariants inside an aggregate.                                         |
| AI Coach                   | AI capability that explains patterns and supports study consistency and reflection without owning official decisions. |
| AI Evaluator               | AI capability that proposes structured rubric-based grades subject to governance.                                     |
| AI Planner                 | AI capability that proposes schedules within approved path, goal and workload constraints.                            |
| AI Predictor               | AI capability supporting score/readiness forecasts with model version and confidence.                                 |
| AI Tutor                   | AI capability that teaches and explains authorized academic content, restricted during protected assessments.         |
| Assessment Blueprint       | Versioned composition and coverage rules used to construct equivalent assessment forms.                               |
| Assessment Definition      | Stable academic identity and purpose of an assessment.                                                                |
| Assessment Delivery        | Scheduled or on-demand availability of an exact form to an eligible audience.                                         |
| Assessment Form            | Exact immutable section and question-version arrangement delivered to learners.                                       |
| Assessment Version         | Immutable rules, blueprint, scoring, timing and release definition for an assessment.                                 |
| Attempt                    | One learner’s authoritative execution of an assessment delivery.                                                      |
| Baseline                   | Initial evidence-supported estimate of starting performance, not a permanent label.                                   |
| Capability                 | A governed function made available by role, scope, course configuration and state.                                    |
| Cohort                     | Operational grouping within a CourseOffering.                                                                         |
| Command                    | Authorized request to change a domain aggregate; it may be rejected.                                                  |
| Competency                 | Versioned measurable skill, subskill or observable capability.                                                        |
| Competency Framework       | Versioned academic hierarchy and shared language to which content, questions, evidence and readiness map.             |
| Course                     | Stable academy-owned preparation product aligned to an exam product.                                                  |
| Course Offering            | Scheduled or commercial delivery of one CourseVersion.                                                                |
| Course Version             | Immutable published definition of course capability and curriculum alignment.                                         |
| Curriculum                 | Published structure of modules, lessons, objectives and activities available in a course version.                     |
| Diagnostic Assessment      | Assessment designed to establish baseline, placement and competency gaps.                                             |
| Domain Event               | Immutable past-tense fact published after a valid domain state change.                                                |
| Enrollment                 | Entitlement linking a student relationship to an exact course offering and pinned academic context.                   |
| Exam Goal                  | Stable identity of the result a learner intends or is required to achieve.                                            |
| Exam Product               | Stable standardized examination family such as IELTS Academic or SAT.                                                 |
| Exam Product Version       | Effective specification of structure, score semantics, timing, tools and policies.                                    |
| Exam Readiness             | Product-specific estimate of proximity to a target result under relevant conditions, reported with confidence.        |
| Exam Simulation            | Exam-fidelity rehearsal using configurable product/version policies over the common delivery kernel.                  |
| Gap Analysis               | Evidence-based identification of competency strengths, weaknesses and priorities relative to a goal.                  |
| Grade Decision             | The effective academic result after applicable objective/AI grading and human review.                                 |
| Grade Run                  | One evaluation execution using fixed input, rubric and grader/model versions.                                         |
| Goal Version               | Immutable target score/date/study-constraint snapshot used by plans and predictions.                                  |
| Instructor Assignment      | Time- and scope-bound academic responsibility for a course, offering or cohort.                                       |
| Intervention Case          | Traceable Student Success workflow responding to an academic or engagement signal.                                    |
| Journey Snapshot           | Immutable point-in-time summary of journey stage and active goal/path/plan/readiness references.                      |
| Learning Activity          | Actionable learning unit with explicit completion semantics.                                                          |
| Learning Completion        | Verified satisfaction of a learning activity’s completion rule.                                                       |
| Learning Path              | Journey-specific academic roadmap through required, optional and conditional activities.                              |
| Learning Workspace         | Personalized, enrollment/journey-scoped experience composed from authoritative domain projections.                    |
| Mastery                    | Modelled estimate of learner capability for a versioned competency, supported by evidence and confidence.             |
| Mock Examination           | User-facing term for an Exam Simulation; not a separate persistence engine.                                           |
| Official Result            | External exam result with source and verification status, compared with a GoalVersion.                                |
| Preparation Journey        | Umbrella coordinating one learner’s effort toward one primary exam objective.                                         |
| Practice Policy            | Versioned rules for adaptive selection, exposure, feedback and evidence treatment.                                    |
| Publication Package        | Immutable manifest of exact academic versions, approvals and quality evidence released together.                      |
| Question Item              | Stable lineage identity of an assessment item.                                                                        |
| Question Version           | Immutable version of prompt, response configuration, answer, explanation and mappings.                                |
| Readiness Confidence       | Reliability of a readiness estimate based on evidence volume, diversity, recency, quality and model certainty.        |
| Readiness Snapshot         | Immutable prediction and target-gap result calculated against a GoalVersion and model version.                        |
| Recommendation             | Time-bound, explainable next-best eligible academic action.                                                           |
| Recommendation Policy      | Versioned candidate, eligibility, ranking and constraint rules.                                                       |
| Response                   | Learner answer to an exact QuestionVersion within an Attempt.                                                         |
| Retake Journey             | New PreparationJourney linked to a prior journey/outcome for another attempt at a goal.                               |
| Role Assignment            | Temporal grant of a named role within a precise academy/resource scope.                                               |
| Score Scale                | Versioned native score dimensions and conversion semantics for an exam product.                                       |
| Sponsor                    | Parent, guardian, employer, school or funder granted limited consent-based visibility.                                |
| Student Relationship       | The person’s lifecycle relationship with an academy; not a course entitlement.                                        |
| Student Success            | Capability monitoring engagement, trajectory and interventions toward target achievement.                             |
| Study Plan                 | Time-based schedule translating a LearningPath into planned activities.                                               |
| Stimulus                   | Reusable versioned passage, audio, image, chart or source shared by questions.                                        |
| Submission                 | Immutable accepted learner work queued for evaluation.                                                                |
| Target Achievement         | Outcome comparison showing whether the verified official result met the defined GoalVersion.                          |
| Timeline                   | Rebuildable chronological projection of selected journey events; not a transactional domain.                          |
| Workspace Projection       | Optimized, audience-specific read model for the LearningWorkspace.                                                    |

# SECTION 18 — ARCHITECTURE DECISION RECORDS (ADR)

These ADRs record binding decisions. A later change requires a superseding ADR that identifies migration and compatibility consequences.

| ADR     | Decision                                                                          | Context                                                                                          | Alternatives Considered                                                           | Trade-offs                                                               | Rationale                                                                                           | Consequences                                                                                |
| ------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| ADR-001 | Preparation Journey is the academic umbrella                                      | Course and enrollment cannot express baseline, goal, path, readiness and outcome across retakes. | Course-centred progress; enrollment-centred progress; one global student profile. | Adds a new aggregate and orchestration complexity.                       | It matches the learner’s real preparation objective while keeping content and entitlement separate. | Every academic activity, evidence and readiness result resolves through a journey.          |
| ADR-002 | Enrollment remains the entitlement source                                         | Journey stage or course membership could be misused as access.                                   | Journey grants access; role grants course access; direct course membership.       | Requires access checks across commands.                                  | Enrollment has explicit offering/version/date/status semantics.                                     | Workspace and delivery require active enrollment even when journey exists.                  |
| ADR-003 | Exam readiness is the primary academic outcome                                    | Completion does not predict standardized-exam success.                                           | Course completion; average score; instructor judgement only.                      | Readiness models require evidence governance and calibration.            | Target-aware, product-specific evidence better represents preparation.                              | Completion remains supporting data; readiness includes confidence and may be insufficient.  |
| ADR-004 | Use separate Student Relationship, Enrollment, Journey and Outcome state machines | One lifecycle chain combines institutional, entitlement, academic and result states.             | Single student status; free-form tags.                                            | More entities and transitions.                                           | Each state has clear owner and avoids contradictory combinations.                                   | Reports join states intentionally; no overloaded status column.                             |
| ADR-005 | Competency Framework is the academic spine                                        | Content, questions and feedback need shared skill semantics.                                     | Free-text tags; question type as skill; course-specific taxonomies.               | Versioned mappings require governance.                                   | Supports adaptive practice, mastery, readiness and cross-content analysis.                          | All high-impact academic objects map to versioned competencies.                             |
| ADR-006 | Diagnostic is a first-class subdomain over the delivery kernel                    | Personalization requires baseline evidence, but a separate engine duplicates delivery.           | Same starting path for all; separate diagnostic tables/engine.                    | Requires diagnostic reports and confidence rules.                        | Reuses secure assessment mechanics while owning placement/gap semantics.                            | Diagnostic assignments, reports and gaps are distinct from ordinary assessment definitions. |
| ADR-007 | Learning Path and Study Plan are separate                                         | Academic route and calendar schedule change for different reasons.                               | One combined plan; only recommendations.                                          | Adds synchronization through versions/events.                            | Path answers what sequence; plan answers when.                                                      | Recommendations navigate within eligible path/plan context.                                 |
| ADR-008 | Learning Workspace is a read/orchestration capability, not source domain          | A dashboard can become duplicate state and business logic.                                       | Separate dashboard per exam; unrestricted JSON page builder.                      | Requires projection freshness and rebuild mechanisms.                    | Typed capabilities preserve consistency and multi-exam scalability.                                 | Workspace writes only via domain commands.                                                  |
| ADR-009 | Use a modular monolith plus asynchronous workers initially                        | Microservices add distributed complexity before organizational need.                             | Traditional monolith; immediate microservices.                                    | Requires strict module boundaries and architecture tests.                | Strong transactions and lower operations cost with scalable AI/media/jobs.                          | Modules may be extracted only against evidence-based criteria.                              |
| ADR-010 | Use one Examination Delivery Kernel                                               | Diagnostics, practice, assessments and mocks share attempts/responses/timing/submission.         | Four independent engines; generic quiz table with no policies.                    | Shared kernel must remain small and stable.                              | Eliminates duplicated security and result logic while allowing policy specialization.               | Exam Simulation remains a domain over the kernel.                                           |
| ADR-011 | Assessment definition, version, blueprint, form and delivery are separate         | Authoring purpose, composition, exact items and availability change independently.               | Single mutable test record.                                                       | More objects and publication steps.                                      | Ensures equivalent forms, historical reproducibility and safe scheduling.                           | Attempts reference exact form and delivery.                                                 |
| ADR-012 | Published academic versions are immutable                                         | Editing used content invalidates historical results.                                             | In-place edits; database snapshots only at attempt time.                          | Creates version proliferation and retirement workflows.                  | Auditability and impact analysis outweigh storage cost.                                             | Corrections supersede or withdraw; no mutable latest references.                            |
| ADR-013 | Core data is relational and normalized                                            | Relationships, uniqueness and authorization cannot be reliably governed in arbitrary JSON.       | JSON-first document model; exam-specific tables.                                  | Some type-specific structures still require validated structured fields. | Relational ownership and constraints support long-term integrity.                                   | JSON is limited to bounded variation and provider metadata.                                 |
| ADR-014 | Shared-schema multi-academy tenancy with RLS                                      | One platform serves multiple academies and needs shared evolution.                               | Database per academy; schema per academy.                                         | Tenant-isolation mistakes are high impact.                               | Shared migrations and reporting reduce cost; RLS plus app authorization mitigates risk.             | Every academy-owned object carries/derives academy scope and has negative tests.            |
| ADR-015 | RBAC and ABAC are combined                                                        | Roles alone cannot express course assignment, enrollment ownership or consent.                   | Global role string; fully custom per-user permissions.                            | Authorization evaluation is more complex.                                | Least privilege requires role plus scope, relationship and state.                                   | Permission matrix and RLS matrix are canonical.                                             |
| ADR-016 | Academic Evidence is an append-oriented ledger                                    | Mastery/readiness need normalized observations from heterogeneous activities.                    | Read raw attempts each time; store one mutable skill percentage.                  | Requires evidence projection and invalidation handling.                  | Traceability and model evolution demand source-level evidence.                                      | Evidence never replaces authoritative attempt/grade records.                                |
| ADR-017 | Mastery and Readiness are separate derived domains                                | Ability by skill and likelihood of reaching a target are different.                              | One progress score; readiness equals average mastery.                             | More calculations and projections.                                       | Readiness needs goals, coverage, fidelity, timing and confidence beyond mastery.                    | Both are versioned snapshots with explicit evidence boundaries.                             |
| ADR-018 | Adaptive practice is rules-first and hybrid-evolvable                             | Random practice does not optimize learning; opaque ML is premature.                              | Random selection; fully opaque ML from launch.                                    | Rules need academic maintenance and may be less personalized initially.  | Explainable policies work before sufficient training data and can later incorporate models.         | Selection rationale, policy and item set are persisted.                                     |
| ADR-019 | Exam Simulation owns fidelity policies, not duplicate persistence                 | Mocks require exam-specific timing/routing/tools but share secure execution.                     | Mock as long assessment only; separate app/table set per exam.                    | Policy packs and fidelity QA add governance.                             | Supports SAT/IELTS/TOEFL/CELPIP and future products configurably.                                   | Session pins policy pack and emits high-fidelity evidence.                                  |
| ADR-020 | AI capabilities are separated by role and authority                               | Grading, tutoring, coaching, planning and prediction have different risks.                       | One general AI assistant; provider-specific direct calls.                         | More policy/model inventories and evaluation suites.                     | Consequential outputs need capability-specific approval and guardrails.                             | Evaluator, Tutor, Coach, Planner and Predictor share gateway but not authority.             |
| ADR-021 | AI provider integration uses anti-corruption adapters                             | Provider models, payloads and policies will change.                                              | Embed provider SDK logic in domains; single provider lock-in.                     | Adapter maintenance and lowest-common-contract risks.                    | Canonical contracts protect academic semantics and enable fallback.                                 | Provider IDs/raw payloads stay in integration/AI records.                                   |
| ADR-022 | LLM output is never the authoritative system of record                            | Generative output may be inconsistent or ungrounded.                                             | Store AI text as final decision; manual-only grading.                             | Structured validation and human review increase latency/cost.            | Grades, recommendations and readiness require deterministic provenance and policy.                  | AI runs feed GradeDecision or advisory explanations through controlled commands.            |
| ADR-023 | Use transactional outbox and at-least-once events                                 | Derived intelligence and notifications must not be lost after commits.                           | In-request side effects; distributed transactions; best-effort jobs.              | Consumers must be idempotent and eventual consistency visible.           | Outbox preserves reliable fact publication without coupling transactions.                           | Event envelope, retries, dead-letter and replay governance are required.                    |
| ADR-024 | Analytics and workspace use rebuildable projections                               | Cross-domain reads are expensive and volatile.                                                   | Direct joins on every screen; analytics tables as truth.                          | Projection lag and rebuild tooling are required.                         | Protects exam workload and enables audience-specific views.                                         | Source domains remain authoritative and freshness is disclosed.                             |
| ADR-025 | Academic publishing is risk-tiered                                                | A lesson PDF and an AI scoring rubric do not carry equal risk.                                   | One draft/publish switch; full pilot for everything.                              | More workflow and staffing requirements.                                 | Review, QA, pilot and approval should match academic consequence.                                   | Tier 3–4 assets require stronger separation, validation and impact handling.                |
| ADR-026 | Question bank uses stable item identity and immutable versions                    | Questions evolve and are reused across forms.                                                    | One mutable question row; table per question type.                                | Versioning and mapping add authoring complexity.                         | Reproducible attempts and item statistics require exact versions.                                   | Type-specific variation is validated without duplicate table families.                      |
| ADR-027 | Sponsor access is consented delegated visibility                                  | Parents/sponsors need progress but not student-equivalent access.                                | Share student login; generic sponsor role; no sponsor support.                    | Field-level policies and age/legal variation add complexity.             | Privacy and trust require relationship-specific access.                                             | Sponsor queries use dedicated projections and are auditable/revocable.                      |
| ADR-028 | Target achievement replaces universal pass/fail                                   | Many exams have no universal pass threshold.                                                     | Boolean passed field for all products.                                            | Reports require goal/source context.                                     | Outcome must compare verified result with individual/institution target.                            | OfficialResult and GoalVersion determine outcome labels.                                    |
| ADR-029 | Soft deletion is selective, not universal                                         | Academic records need preservation; blanket deleted flags hide semantics.                        | Hard delete everything; soft delete every table.                                  | Multiple lifecycle verbs and retention rules.                            | Retire, withdraw, invalidate and supersede accurately describe business meaning.                    | Hard delete is limited to unused drafts/privacy-eligible data after checks.                 |
| ADR-030 | Phase 0.5 is a mandatory gate before physical design                              | Without canonical contracts, teams will encode conflicting meanings.                             | Start coding from Phase 0; evolve schema ad hoc.                                  | Adds upfront review time.                                                | A 5–10 year platform requires agreed ownership, states, events, rules and security.                 | Phase 1 cannot receive unconditional approval until critical gates below close.             |
| ADR-031 | QTI/learning standards are interoperability boundaries, not database schemas      | External content exchange is useful but internal requirements are richer.                        | Adopt external schema as canonical; ignore standards.                             | Adapters require mapping and extension policies.                         | Internal model remains optimized while imports/exports remain possible.                             | Imported content always enters provenance and publishing workflows.                         |
| ADR-032 | Official result verification is separate from self-report                         | Outcome calibration and sponsor reporting need provenance.                                       | Treat every entered score as verified; disallow self-report.                      | Verification workflow and evidence handling add cost.                    | Separating status preserves utility without overstating trust.                                      | Readiness calibration uses verified results according to model policy.                      |
| ADR-033 | Operational priority favors autosave/submission over intelligence jobs            | Concurrent exams require durable writes even during AI/analytics load.                           | Equal queue/database priority; synchronous AI grading.                            | Requires workload classes and backpressure.                              | Losing a student response is more damaging than delayed prediction.                                 | Workers, quotas and monitoring preserve critical-path capacity.                             |
| ADR-034 | Architecture extraction is evidence-driven                                        | Microservice boundaries should follow scaling/team/regulatory needs.                             | Never extract; split every domain immediately.                                    | Later extraction requires contracts and data migration.                  | Canonical domain/events make later extraction possible without paying early cost.                   | AI/media/notification/analytics are likely candidates, not commitments.                     |

# SECTION 19 — PHASE 1 READINESS REVIEW

## 19.1 Cross-section consistency review

The canonical model is internally consistent on the following critical points:

- The student’s institutional relationship, entitlement, preparation context and outcome are separate.
- Course remains the content/product container; PreparationJourney is the academic umbrella.
- GoalVersion is the target context for readiness and planning.
- Diagnostic, practice, assessment and simulation share one Delivery Kernel.
- Competency Framework connects content, questions, evidence, mastery and readiness.
- Academic Evidence, Mastery, Readiness and Academic Intelligence have distinct authority.
- Workspace, timeline and reports are projections.
- Published content, model and policy versions are immutable.
- AI roles have separate authority and governance.
- Permission, RLS, command and event models align around domain ownership.
- Migration uses mappings and staged cutover rather than preserving legacy table semantics.

## 19.2 Remaining weak boundaries requiring Phase 1 design discipline

1. **Preparation Journey vs Goal:** Journey coordinates an active goal but must not absorb target-version history.
2. **Learning Path vs Recommendation:** Path owns roadmap; Recommendation owns ranked next action. Recommendations cannot silently rewrite paths.
3. **Study Planning vs Student Success:** Planning schedules work; Student Success manages interventions and adherence signals.
4. **Assessment Definition vs Exam Simulation:** Simulation may reference forms but owns fidelity policy and session incidents.
5. **Submission & Grading vs AI Academic Intelligence:** Grading owns effective decisions; AI owns model execution and validated proposals.
6. **Academic Operations vs owner domains:** Operations owns queues, assignments and orchestration only.
7. **Academic Intelligence vs source domains:** Intelligence owns metrics/projections, never corrections to truth.
8. **File Asset ownership:** Retention and access must derive from the owning business object without circular deletion.
9. **Global vs academy content:** Licensing, copying and override semantics require explicit policy before cross-academy sharing.
10. **Official results and readiness calibration:** Verification levels and permitted training/evaluation use require governance.

## 19.3 Scalability risks

| Risk                         | Why it matters                                              | Required treatment before/within Phase 1                                                        |
| ---------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Autosave write concentration | Thousands of concurrent attempts produce hot paths          | Define partition/access key, idempotency/sequence contract, load target and failure recovery    |
| Evidence/event fan-out       | One result may trigger many projections                     | Outbox partitions, idempotent consumers, replay and lag monitoring                              |
| RLS complexity               | Deep policies can become slow or incorrect                  | Resolve scope efficiently, index policy predicates, test negative paths and profile query plans |
| Version proliferation        | Long-lived content creates many immutable versions          | Archival/read-model strategy, manifests and dependency lookup                                   |
| Workspace composition        | Cross-domain synchronous aggregation can be slow            | Precomputed projections and freshness policy                                                    |
| Question selection           | Large pools plus exposure/mastery constraints are expensive | Candidate indexes, staged filtering, selector cache and deterministic persistence               |
| AI cost/latency              | Writing/speaking and tutoring can dominate cost             | Queue classes, quotas, model routing, budget dashboards and fallback                            |
| Media processing             | Audio/video uploads are large and bursty                    | Direct signed upload, asynchronous scan/transcode, quotas and retention                         |
| Academic analytics           | Heavy cohort/item queries can affect exams                  | Event projections, materialized summaries, replica/warehouse threshold                          |
| Migration dual-running       | Divergent truth and repeated defects                        | Short domain-specific dual-run windows and reconciliation ownership                             |
| Tenant growth                | Shared schema can amplify noisy-neighbour risk              | Per-academy quotas, workload isolation and future sharding criteria                             |
| Event schema evolution       | Many consumers can block change                             | Versioned events, contract tests and consumer inventory                                         |

## 19.4 Critical issues that are not yet resolved

This logical specification intentionally does not invent business or regulatory decisions that require named owners and evidence. The following are **Phase 1 blockers**:

1. **Named domain owners and approvers** must be assigned to every core and supporting domain.
2. **Exam-product specification packs** for the first launch products must be approved, including sections, timing, scoring, tools, accommodations and result semantics.
3. **Competency Framework Version 1** must be defined for the first launch product and mapped to initial course content/question taxonomy.
4. **Readiness Model Version 1** must define minimum evidence, score conversion, confidence, states and validation plan.
5. **Adaptive Practice Policy Version 1** must define selection factors, item exposure, review rules and mastery update.
6. **Academic publishing tiers and required reviewers** must be approved and staffed.
7. **AI governance policy** must define approved capabilities, benchmark datasets, review thresholds, provider data retention, appeal and incident handling.
8. **Privacy, consent, minors and sponsor policy** must be approved for the operating jurisdictions.
9. **Data retention schedule** must specify periods and legal holds by data class.
10. **Complete legacy inventory and source-to-target mapping** must be produced and sampled.
11. **Non-functional targets** must be formally accepted: concurrency, autosave durability, latency, availability, RPO/RTO, AI turnaround and cost budgets.
12. **Permission and RLS matrices** must be reviewed by security, academic operations and product owners with negative test scenarios.
13. **Course/global-content ownership and licensing policy** must be defined.
14. **Official-result verification policy** and use for model calibration must be approved.
15. **Physical database design review process** must be established so Phase 1 SQL conforms to aggregate, versioning, indexing and RLS requirements.

## 19.5 Missing or deferred business-rule areas

The 270-rule catalogue is sufficient for logical architecture but must be extended during product-specific design for:

- Exact IELTS/SAT/TOEFL/CELPIP timing and scoring rules.
- Accessibility and accommodation rules per examination.
- Commercial/payment/refund rules if billing is in V2 scope.
- Attendance/live-class rules if these affect entitlement or success.
- Content licensing and geographic restrictions.
- Appeals, academic misconduct and proctoring procedures.
- Certificates and claims permitted by academy policy.
- Data residency and cross-border AI processing.
- Retention periods and age/guardian rules.
- Model calibration thresholds and acceptable human-AI agreement.

These extensions must use the existing domain and rule structure; they do not justify duplicate engines.

| Migration                | Order, mapping, compatibility and risks defined                                       | Blocked                        | Legacy inventory and rehearsed mappings not yet supplied               |

| NFR and operations       | Scalability risks identified                                                          | Blocked                        | Measurable SLOs, load profiles and recovery targets require approval   |

## 19.7 Phase 1 decision

# PHASE 1 IS NOT YET APPROVED.

The canonical logical architecture is sufficiently mature to begin **review, prototyping and physical-design preparation**, but production Platform Foundation implementation should not begin until all Critical issues in Section 19.4 have named owners, approved decisions and traceable evidence.

The recommended governance decision is:

- Approve this document as the **Phase 0.5 Architecture Approval Draft**.
- Open a short Architecture Closure workstream for the 15 blockers.
- Permit non-production spikes only where they validate a blocker and create no irreversible schema.
- Conduct the final Phase 0.5 gate review.
- Approve Phase 1 only when all critical checklist rows are Pass or have formally accepted, time-bound exceptions documented by ADR.

## 19.8 Required final sign-offs

- CTO / Chief Enterprise Architect.
- Product Owner.
- Academic Director or Examination Product Owner.
- Database Architect.
- Security and Privacy Owner.
- AI Governance Owner.
- Platform/DevOps Owner.
- Migration Owner.
- QA/Test Architecture Owner.

No single technical lead should unilaterally waive academic validity, privacy, AI governance or migration-reconciliation blockers.
